The 10 Best Netflix Original Thriller Movies, Ranked

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As Netflix continues to be one of the most successful and prominent distributors of both film and television in the modern era of streaming services and digital media, they have made waves by releasing a massive amount of content in each distinct genre of filmmaking. One of the most prominent and impactful genres that Netflix has made its mark on is the thriller genre, releasing a wide array of not only high-octane action thrillers but also slow and methodical crime thrillers and other explorations of suspense.

Netflix has largely cemented itself as a hub for enticing and highly engaging original thriller films from a wide array of both legendary and rising filmmakers and stars in the industry. While other streaming services have their fair share of great original thrillers to offer, in terms of sheer mass and volume of great thriller experiences, Netflix has the competition beat. Several of these thrillers are so effective that they are in conversation as some of the best thrillers in recent memory.

While Anna Kendrick is more commonly known for her work in various comedy films like Pitch Perfect and A Simple Favor, her directorial debut with Woman of the Hour is a dark and unnerving serial killer film. Based on a real-life story, the film follows the events surrounding the time when a notorious serial killer found himself competing as a contestant on the beloved TV game show The Dating Game during the 1970s. On top of directing the film, Kendrick portrays the female contestant playing alongside this unknown mass murderer.

Woman of the Hour does a great job of setting up the pieces and building up to the main event game show, making sure that its core moments are oozing with tension and dread. The film shows not only the life of the innocent female contestant before her time in the game, but also various murders committed by Rodney (Daniel Zovatto) to get a sense of his pure evil nature despite his charming demeanor. Beyond the actual story being told, however, there is a powerful and deeply impactful subtext about the power dynamics of the era and how ignorance leads to pure evil going unpunished.

The first of what would end up being several Stephen King adaptations by modern horror auteur Mike Flanagan, Gerald's Game does a great job of balancing both the horror and the rising anxiety and tension of its premise. The film sees a married couple having fun at an isolated house for a holiday, getting up to some erotic sexual escapades. However, after Jessie (Carla Gugino) is handcuffed to the bed, her husband Gerald (Bruce Greenwood) suddenly dies of a heart attack, leaving her stuck with little hope of rescue.

Gerald's Game does a brilliant job of translating the tension and dread of the original King novel to the big screen, especially amplified by its powerful lead performance from Gugino. While it certainly plays with elements of horror filmmaking, it is much more in line with a psychological thriller, following the dread and inner demons that Jessie faces while in such a painful predicament. The film quickly cemented Flanagan as the go-to visionary for modern King adaptations, further amplified by his work on films like Doctor Sleep and The Life of Chuck.

While acclaimed writer/director Charlie Kaufman isn't nearly as well known for his work on thrillers, I'm Thinking of Ending Things acts as an ingenious combination of his signature style through the lens of a psychological thriller. The film follows a young woman (Jessie Buckley) who goes on a trip with her boyfriend (Jesse Plemons) to meet his parents (Toni Collette and David Thewlis). However, the film proves to be much more dynamic and complicated than this simple premise would have one believe, as the story begins intersecting with seemingly unconnected footage of a school janitor (Guy Boyd) going to work.

Much like Kaufman's other work, I'm Thinking of Ending Things is entirely defined by its subtext and deeper subliminal meanings, and while this can make for an inaccessible experience for traditional audiences, those in touch with the subtext will find a rich story of pain, humanity, and a yearning soul. The film is exceptional when it comes to the finer details and unnerving atmosphere of its story, amplifying these feelings of things being slightly off and uncomfortable until it reaches its confusing yet highly engaging third act.

A period-piece crime thriller that combines the culture of rural America with a more raw and guttural look at the people and pains of post-World War II, The Devil All the Time follows a rarely explored piece of American history. The film follows the ins and outs of the town of Knockmstiff, Ohio, and its neighboring backwoods, as various sinister characters and devilish opportunities begin to crop up around young Arvin Russell (Tom Holland). Amidst the pain and difficulties that he faces, Arvin is dedicated to fighting these evil forces that threaten him and his family.

More than anything else, The Devil All the Time's greatest strengths come from how it brings to life the conniving yet heartfelt world of rural America in the mid-20th century. Various characters breathe life into the world in their own distinct ways, whether it be through innocence and charm or unsuspecting evil and malice. Holland does a great job with one of his rare solely dramatic roles, yet it's easily Robert Pattinson's performance as the manipulative Southern priest Reverend Preston Teagarden that steals the show and makes the film a must-watch thriller.

The Night Comes for Us brings together a lot of the same talent from the acclaimed Indonesian action thriller series The Raid to create another raw and unrestrained action thriller that is basically a part of The Raid franchise in everything but name. The violent martial arts film follows an elite Triad assassin (Joe Taslim) finding himself in the crosshairs of his former employers after sparing a young girl's life during a massacre. Most notably, he is forced to face off against his friend Arian (Iko Uwais), who is attempting to kill him in an attempt to gain power among the gangs.

The Night Comes for Us features a lot of the same highlights and entertaining aspects of The Raid series, with cutthroat action and top-of-the-line choreography amplifying the action for true catharsis. Its relatively simple plot leads the way to some of the most entertaining and vicious action imaginable, providing a distinct style that is different from any of the action thrillers being made in the U.S.

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

A sensual and conniving erotic thriller that delves into the difficulties of balancing a secret relationship while working at the same job, Fair Play gets into all sorts of tension and psychological warfare as its broken characters shift in and out of love and competition. The film follows Emily Meyers (Phoebe Dynevor) and Luke Edmunds (Alden Ehrenreich), a duo of analysts working at a cutthroat Manhattan hedge fund who have been fostering a passionate relationship in secret from their co-workers. However, after Luke engages Emily, they find themselves at a crossroads when Emily receives a promotion instead of Luke, creating a sting of jealousy in their relationship.

Fair Play is largely elevated by the inherent chemistry and dynamics between its two leads, with Dynevor and Ehrenreich having a flexible and ever-evolving relationship that ranges from undying passion to visceral anger. Their performances prove to elevate the thrills and tension of the film as a whole, making each moment and twist feel more palpable and impactful than the last. It delves into the toxic mentality that leads to relationships falling apart, and how some people simply can't imagine not being the breadwinner in a relationship.

Carry-On harkens back to a simpler time of action thriller filmmaking, where simply having an engaging premise with a unique setting and sleek execution was enough to elevate a film to be as entertaining as possible. The film follows Ethan Kopek (Taron Egerton), a TSA officer working on Christmas Eve who finds himself racing against the clock to stop a mysterious traveler (Jason Bateman) from getting a dangerous item onto a flight. It quickly becomes a game of cat and mouse between the two as they attempt to outsmart one another and stay one step ahead.

Considering how director Jaume Collet-Serra released a wide array of great action thrillers throughout the 2010s, Carry-On is not only a return to form for him as a director, but also a return to form for the genre as a whole. The airport setting simply works perfectly for this type of race-against-time thriller, with compelling characters and an array of twists and turns making the film a very entertaining crowd-pleaser. Audiences certainly appreciated this return to form, as Carry-On quickly became one of Netflix's biggest successes of recent memory.

While Netflix is widely celebrated for its various American film releases, there are also a great number of international features that are released by the company worldwide, with one of the most underrated being the Polish thriller Operation Hyacinth. The film is based on the events surrounding the titular secret mass operation of the Polish communist police, where they attempted to create a national database of all homosexuals in Poland. The film follows a police officer delving into a dark rabbit hole after being personally dissatisfied with the result of a murder investigation.

Operation Hyacinth has all the elements of a truly great crime thriller, with a large emphasis on mystery and hidden information from its protagonist and the heightened fear of getting to the bottom of the truth. Its prospects as a Polish film have limited the spread of its influence among American audiences, yet the film is so effective in its filmmaking that it easily deserves to be in the conversation of one of Netflix's best thrillers to date.

A frequent trend that has defined action thrillers of recent memory has been the John Wick-style of film, where a single vengeful man takes down hordes of enemies all by themselves. While it certainly has a lot of the same benefits as this style of thriller, Rebel Ridge is a highly engaging twist on the premise, seeing a situation escalate to the point of no return. The film follows a former Marine (Aaron Pierre) forced to confront corruption in a small town after their local law enforcement unjustly seizes a bag of cash that he needs to post his cousin's bail.

Rebel Ridge excels not only in terms of energizing and exceptionally choreographed action, but also as a simple yet painfully relevant story of the abuse of power and corruption in local law enforcement. Pierre gives a committed lead performance that leans into both his stern capabilities as a former soldier and his cool, calm demeanor under all sorts of pressure. Not a second in the film goes by that isn't impactful on the audience, making the film not only one of Netflix's best thrillers but also one of their best original action movies.

David Fincher has been a widely beloved and impactful director when it comes to the thriller genre, with films like Se7en, Zodiac, and Gone Girl still being considered some of the most acclaimed thrillers of all time. However, The Killer has proven to be one of the most unique and dynamic thrillers that the director has ever released, actively going against the grain and commenting on his style and influence in the genre. The film follows an assassin (Michael Fassbender) who is forced to face off against his employers after a catastrophic miss on his latest mission.

From the outset, The Killer seems like it would fall into place and feel similar to Fincher's previous thrillers, following a stern and calculated protagonist distancing himself from the horrors of murder. However, the film actively goes against this grain of thinking, satirizing this restrained mindset and showing the difficulties of living such an isolated, perfectionist lifestyle. It brilliantly comments and critiques Fincher's own work while also evolving it to the modern era, making for a distinct evolution of his storytelling style and the must-watch thriller of the decade.

Duran & Osmand spark Celtic into life - but where does that leave Hogh?

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran were key as Celtic turned the game on its head

With an hour gone at Parkhead on Thursday, hope in the stands was in short supply.

Celtic were 1-0 down against Besiktas and heading towards a fourth straight defeat in this season's Europa League.

Manager Martin O'Neill turned to his bench for a spark, bringing on Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran to replace Sebastian Tounekti and James Forrest out wide.

Osmand's first touch nestled in the bottom corner of Alexander Nubel's goal, just 43 seconds after the 20-year-old attacker came on.

All of a sudden, Celtic had a threat in behind and Besiktas looked rattled.

Then, on 77 minutes, O'Neill withdrew Kasper Hogh after another underwhelming display from the club record signing and moved Duran through the middle.

Osmand played Duran through with a delightful pass and the Colombian rounded Nubel before slotting Celtic into the lead.

Orkun Kokcu levelled for Besiktas with a fine strike, but Duran had the final say two minutes later, controlling Kieran Tierney's scuffed shot and beating Nubel from close range to send the home support into ecstasy.

Late Duran double downs Besiktas to give Celtic first Europa League win

Latest Celtic news, analysis and fan views

"Everything changed with the subs; Osmand coming on and Duran going through the middle," former Celtic midfielder Stiliyan Petrov said on TNT Sports. "They freed up space for Nygren and Baur. It was a really positive 30 minutes."

Not since Johannes Edvaldsson against Jeunesse d'Esch in the European Cup in September 1977 had a Celtic substitute scored twice in Europe (excluding qualifiers).

"Osmand came on, first touch put the ball in the net and that's really difficult, he can definitely score a goal. Duran has been fabulous for us all season no matter where he plays," O'Neill said of his goalscorers.

The Northern Irishman was asked whether it was a case of inspired substitutions or rather evidence that he should have started his best players.

"I'm not standing up for myself, not at all," he said.

"It was in design to do exactly what it did. We've played nine games in 25 days, some players needed a breather and you can get re-energised from coming on the field.

"I'm going to say that that was our plan and those players were going to come on the pitch at some point in the game."

'Celtic character shone through big time' - O'Neill

The contrast between Celtic's attacking play with Hogh leading the line and with Duran through the middle was stark.

The Â£11m Dane's effort cannot be questioned. He continued to battle to try to make things happen, but since arriving from Bodo/Glimt in the summer, he has now failed to score in 16 of his 17 Celtic appearances.

In 77 minutes against Besiktas, he didn't have a single shot and managed just one touch in the opposition box.

Duran, meanwhile, played just half an hour and scored two goals, had two shots, and registered four touches in the Besiktas box.

O'Neill has previously said he is unsure of his best team, but it is now obvious that Duran is Celtic's best option through the middle.

Despite Hogh's price tag, it feels inevitable he will be among the Celtic substitutes for high-stakes games.

"It'll be tough for Hogh," Hearts defender Craig Halkett said on TNT Sports. "He was the big-money singing but it's not coming off for him.

"As a player you need to keep your head down, keep working hard and hope it turns."

Osmand too appears to have pushed himself to the front of the queue to start off the left. The former Fulham man is direct and evidently has an eye for goal, with a happy knack for scoring in big games.

"This is a great team we're playing against," Osmand told TNT Sports. "Camilo [Duran] did amazing, he's such a poacher. Credit to him.

"It's a lot harder starting games. The team dug in, stayed strong and we got our rewards.

"[The goal] caught me off guard, but I'm just glad I caught it nice. I just want to come on and make something happen. So happy with tonight."

ASCAP Unveils Mental Health Initiative for Members in Partnership With Project Healthy Minds

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Through the new collaboration, ASCAP members will be connected with mental health providers offering talk therapy, psychiatric services and more.

ASCAP and Project Healthy Minds are collaborating on an initiative to provide the PRO’s songwriter, composer and music publisher members with access to a comprehensive ecosystem of mental health services, therapy options and wellness tools, it was announced Thursday (Nov. 5). The effort is meant to support creators at every stage of their careers and is an outgrowth of ASCAP’s wellness initiative launched in 2019, which earlier this year expanded to include a telehealth benefit through MD Live for urgent care consultations, plus dermatology and additional health services.

“Creativity thrives when creators are supported — in mind, body, and spirit,” Nick Lehman, ASCAP executive vp/chief strategy and digital officer, said in a statement. “Music creators put their everything into the music we love, yet trying to find quality, affordable support shouldn’t feel like everything is stacked against them in an opaque healthcare system. By teaming up with Project Healthy Minds, we are delivering direct access to vetted care — removing friction so our members get meaningful support, whenever and however they need it.”

According to the announcement, the initiative will allow ASCAP members to privately connect with mental health providers that offer such services as talk therapy and psychiatry; peer-led and expert-moderated support groups; and self-care apps and digital tools.

“Songwriters have helped millions of people feel less alone, and this partnership makes sure they have that same support in return,” Project Healthy Minds founder/CEO Phillip Schermer said in a statement. “We are thrilled to give ASCAP’s members a simple way to find care, whether that’s a therapist, a support group or a tool they can use on the road.”

Information on how to access the program is available on the ASCAP member access portal.

Airbnb is working on an AI travel agent

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The travel company is planning an “AI-native” trip-planning experience that’s set to roll out next year.

The travel company is planning an “AI-native” trip-planning experience that’s set to roll out next year.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Airbnb plans to roll out an AI agent in the first half of 2027 that will help users plan and book travel, the company said in its Q3 2026 earnings shareholder letter released today.

“Today’s AI agents are good at simple, routine tasks, like making a restaurant reservation or scheduling an appointment,” Airbnb said. “But planning a trip on Airbnb is different.” The company argued that its experience matching hosts and guests, its current role in being part of reservations already, and a “new interface” built “specifically for travel” gives it an edge.

Here’s how Airbnb says that new interface will work:

We don’t think a traditional chatbot is the right way to plan a trip. We’re developing a custom-designed, AI-native experience that builds the interface around your question. Rather than a chat window, our agent draws on a library of hundreds of travel-specific interface elements—maps, photos, comparisons, calendars, itineraries—and composes them in real time to match what you’re looking for. Our agent will help guests explore options, make decisions with the people they’re traveling with, and take action directly in Airbnb.

Airbnb co-founder and CEO Brian Chesky has said before that the company plans to launch an agent in 2027, telling the Skift Global Forum that it would have an interface that’s “much richer than a chatbot.”

In its shareholder letter, Airbnb also said that AI is “the best thing to ever happen to Airbnb” and has changed how it develops products and operates.

Pope begins lengthy trip to Latin America weeks after lung cancer surgery

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Pope Leo is travelling to Uruguay, Argentina and Peru

Pope Leo XIV is beginning a lengthy trip to Latin America just three weeks after surgery for lung cancer - a tour that includes a much-anticipated "homecoming" to Peru where he was a priest for many years.

The Vatican says no extraordinary measures are being taken in relation to the Pope's health, describing him as a "71-year-old who has undergone an operation" not someone with a "permanent medical condition".

It was Leo himself who revealed last month that doctors had discovered a tumour during a routine scan. The growth was removed along with the lower lobe of his right lung and his medical team then confirmed the cancer had not spread.

At a briefing in Rome ahead of the trip, which takes in Uruguay and Argentina as well as Peru, a spokeswoman stressed that Pope Leo had "fantastic stamina" and was "extremely enthusiastic" about the schedule which he had "personally confirmed... with no changes".

"The Vatican's message with this trip is, 'that was a short episode and now we're back on track'," Professor Massimo Faggioli of Villanova University told the BBC, pointing out that the Pope has had a busy return to work since his surgery.

"We have seen him in these last few days making speeches, audiences and so on. He seems in great shape. That was important."

On Friday Pope Leo presided over the annual memorial Mass for cardinals and bishops who have died

The 12-day tour would be demanding for anyone, though. It incorporates multiple long-haul and local flights and covers thousands of miles from the Amazon to the Andes.

The starting point will be Uruguay, where the number of Catholics has fallen away steeply in recent years and which is getting its first Papal visit in four decades.

Leo then heads for Argentina, homeland of Pope Francis, his predecessor.

Father Fernando Laguna said his heart was filled with joy when the Pope's visit to Argentina was announced

"This visit is also a homage to Francis, who didn't make it to Argentina [as pope]," says Father Fernando Laguna, the rector of Santa Maria Addolorata, Argentina's national church in Rome.

"When this visit by Pope Leo was announced, it filled all of our hearts with joy," he told the BBC. "I always thought he would go, despite his illness. I don't think he ever thought of calling it off!"

The priest is installing a big screen inside the church in Rome to allow parishioners to follow all the key moments of the trip from afar. The building is already decked out with posters declaring Pope Leo is welcome in Argentina. "We're waiting for you!", one proclaims.

'Welcome Pope Leo' reads this poster in the Argentinian church in Rome

This leg of the Pope's journey - which includes meetings with heads of state as well as religious events - could be the most politically sensitive. President Javier Milei once called Pope Francis "evil on earth" and a "communist" for promoting a doctrine of social justice to help the poor. In turn, Argentina's bishops have criticised Milei's harsh austerity measures, making sweeping cuts to social spending.

But Milei has already welcomed Pope Leo, writing in a post on X that Argentina awaits "with immense joy". Millions are expected to turn out to greet him.

The highlight of the trip is undoubtedly saved for last, when Pope Leo reaches Peru - described by the Vatican as a return to his roots.

"He knows these streets," is how a spokeswoman put it.

Worshippers at Chiclayo cathedral in Peru were delighted when their former bishop became Pope in 2025

Born in the US, the then Robert Prevost spent almost 20 years in Peru, on and off, and even has Peruvian citizenship. That time is seen as fundamental to shaping his papacy.

His schedule includes a return to the diocese of Chiclayo where he was ordained bishop in 2014 and remained until Francis moved him to the Vatican almost a decade later.

He will also travel to Cusco, former capital of the Inca Empire, to say Mass more than 3,000m above sea level before heading to Pucallpa in the Amazon.

In all three countries, the Vatican says the Pope will have meetings with survivors of clerical sexual abuse - an issue which clouded a 2018 visit to Chile and Peru by his predecessor.

"This is how the papacy reconnects with Latin America after Francis, whose visits to the region were not always seamless," is how Professor Faggioli sees it.

He believes Pope Leo has a strong sense of the unity of the Americas as one continent. "Democracies are in trouble there, so there are so many things he can address," he suggests.

"The big question then is the United States. There's no question he wants to go. So will he travel when [President Donald] Trump is still in office? I'm sure they're thinking about this."

Meta, Google, and TikTok sue California AG over teen social media law

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Meta, Google, and TikTok have each filed lawsuits against California Attorney General Rob Bonta in an attempt to block a law that restricts social media features it deems addictive for users under 16.

California Governor Gavin Newsom signed the bill, known as AB 1709, into law in September, and it’s slated to take effect at the start of the new year.

The law prohibits social platforms from offering autoplay videos and personalized feeds to users under 16, and it requires the platforms to delete these users’ accounts if they don’t turn those features off. If platforms do not comply, they could be liable for fines of up to $50,000 per affected user under 16 years old.

Meta claims that AB 1709 violates the First Amendment by overriding its editorial decisions about how to display content, adding that Section 230 preempts the law. TikTok and Google made similar arguments.

“Meta currently organizes its feeds of content to include personalized content recommendations and to automatically play media, [so] the Act would make it unlawful for Meta to disseminate speech to minors altogether, and would require Meta to delete minors’ accounts, unless it changes these practices,” the company wrote in its lawsuit.

In an emailed statement, a TikTok spokesperson added, “AB 1709 is a blatantly unconstitutional restriction on free expression that erodes the long-standing and protected practice of curating content based on user preferences, which book stores, TV networks and streaming services all use.”

AB 1709 follows a trend of legislation around the world that seeks to curb teen social media use in an attempt to improve teen mental health outcomes. However, digital safety experts often caution against these laws because they can inadvertently compromise the privacy of all platform users, including adults, by requiring age verification checks that collect more user data.

“AB 1709 compels platforms to collect even more information from every user, invading everyone’s privacy and frustrating our ability to remain anonymous online,” EFF Associate Director of State Affairs Rindala Alajaji said in a press briefing. “Online surveillance by social media companies is harmful, and yet this bill requires even more of it, further concentrating power in the hands of tech companies rather than actually protecting users.”

Alajaji added that teenagers’ use of social media use has been instrumental in enabling impactful digital activism, citing the Parkland shooting survivors’ March for our Lives and climate activist Greta Thunberg’s global reach.

“As a functional ban on social media for youth under 16, AB 1709 cuts young people off from essential information and experiences, silencing the very voices it claims to protect,” Alajaji said.

These platforms are primed to fight this California law as they navigate similar legal battles in various countries. That’s especially true for Meta, which agreed to pay a settlement of up to $18 billion to resolve children’s safety claims brought by 29 U.S. states.

In December 2025, Australia became the first country to ban social media for users under 16 altogether. France passed a similar law, but its top court struck it down, citing concerns about violations of free expression rights.

These platforms also argue that the law would affect parents’ ability to make decisions about their teens’ internet use through parental controls.

A Google spokesperson noted that autoplay on YouTube is turned off for minors by default, and that parents have the ability to turn off their teens’ access to Shorts altogether.

“We believe parents are best positioned to make the right decisions for their families, which is why we offer a range of protections and tools that put parents in control,” a Google spokesperson said. “Overly broad laws like this actually break the systems we’ve built to help kids find high-quality, age-appropriate videos.”

A representative for Attorney General Bonta said that his office is reviewing the filings and is committed to “defending California’s commonsense laws to protect kids online.”

Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 5 novembre

INTER, LO SCHIAVO NUOVO DIRECTOR OF FOOTBALL DEVELOPMENT. ROMA, NEL MIRINO UN ESTERNO: PIACCIONO GITTENS E KVERNADZE. IL MILAN PAGHERÀ 3,5 MILIONI DI EURO PER AVERE KROSCHE. JUVENTUS, PER IL FUTURO OCCHI SU KOVACS. NAPOLI, MCTOMINAY CHIEDE TROPPO: RINNOVO DIFFICILE. IL TORINO SEGUE MIKOLAJEWSKI DEL PARMA.

La notizia era già nell'aria, ma adesso è arrivata anche l'ufficialità: Simone Lo Schiavo è il nuovo Director of Football Development dell'Inter. A renderlo noto è lo stesso club nerazzurro attraverso un comunicato sul proprio sito, nel quale lo ha accolto.

Il mercato di gennaio sarà fondamentale per la Roma. La priorità individuata dalla dirigenza è acquistare un esterno offensivo in grado di garantire imprevedibilità. Tra i nomi seguiti figurano Jamie Gittens del Chelsea, Endrick del Real Madrid, Jamie Leweling dello Stoccarda e Giorgi Kvernadze del Frosinone. In particolare, scrive oggi La Gazzetta dello Sport, quello del talento in forza ai Blues è un pro­filo che piace e che era stato già inse­guito nella sessione estiva.

Il Milan è pronto a pagare la clausola di risoluzione automatica per permettere a Markus Krosche di lasciare l'Eintracht Francoforte e diventare l'Head of Football dei rossoneri. Il Diavolo verserà nelle casse dei tedeschi 3,5 milioni di euro, il dirigente sarà operativo dall'1 gennaio 2027.

La Juventus ha sfidato l'AZ di Bendeguz Kovacs, attaccante del club olandese che i torinesi seguono con attenzione. Già la scorsa estate i bianconeri avevano provato a prenderlo a zero, ma il rinnovo fino al 2029 ha frenato la trattativa. A riportarlo è Tuttosport.

Scott McTominay chiedeva al Napoli 12 milioni di euro all'anno per rinnovare, poi però con il passare del tempo il centrocampista ha abbassato le proprie pretese, arrivando fino a 10. L'impressione è che a 8-9 si potrebbe chiudere l'accordo per il rinnovo, ma difficilmente De Laurentiis accetterà certe cifre e di conseguenza in estate la cessione resta un'ipotesi.

Il Torino continua a seguire l’attaccante polacco Daniel Mikolajewski, classe 2006 che sta facendo molto bene con la maglia del Lucerna, ma di proprietà del Parma. La valutazione in questi mesi è aumentata in maniera considerevole, si parla infatti non a caso di una cifra tra gli 8 e i 10 milioni di euro.

KANE RINNOVA COL BAYERN, RAPHINHA CON IL BARCELLONA. ALISSON VERSO L’ADDIO AL LIVERPOOL: CI SONO DUE CLUB BRASILIANO. EMERY-ASTON VILLA, PRONTO IL RINNOVO

Due rinnovi importanti per due top club europei: il Bayern Monaco ha infatti annunciato il rinnovo di Harry Kane che ha firmato fino al 30 giugno 2029. Il Pallone d’Oro 2026 ha scelto di proseguire la sua avventura in Baviera, dove dal suo arrivo nell’estate 2023 è diventato il simbolo del nuovo corso del club tedesco. Il Barcellona blinda uno dei protagonisti della sua nuova era. Il club catalano ha annunciato il rinnovo di contratto di Raphael Dias Belloli, meglio conosciuto come Raphinha, che ha prolungato il proprio accordo fino al 30 giugno 2030. Una scelta che certifica il peso raggiunto dal brasiliano all'interno del progetto blaugrana, dopo un percorso iniziato nell'estate del 2022.

Il futuro di Alisson Becker sarà lontano dal Liverpool con cui andrà a scadenza di contratto al termine della stagione. Sul portiere brasiliano potrebbe esserci nuovamente l’interesse della Juventus, che l’aveva seguito in estate, anche se al momento il Brasile sembra essere un’opzione più concreta. Il giocatore piace infatti molto al Flamengo, nonostante l’età non più giovanissima del portiere e l’ingaggio elevato, e all’Internacional Porto Alegre, club nel quale Alisson è cresciuto e a cui è rimasto fortemente legato, avendo in passato già espresso il desiderio di farvi ritorno.

Unai Emery è vicino a blindare il suo futuro all'Aston Villa e a spegnere definitivamente le voci su un possibile interesse del Manchester United. Il tecnico spagnolo, protagonista della rinascita del club negli ultimi quattro anni, è pronto a firmare un nuovo accordo (quello attuale scade nel 2029) con i Villans dopo i colloqui avanzati avuti mercoledì con il presidente Nassef Sawiris.

Ocarina Of Time's Ganondorf Called "One Of The Biggest Miscasts In Gaming"

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

Matt Mercer is one of the world's most accomplished voice actors. He's held dozens of roles across TV, film, and games, appearing in series like Attack on Titan, Resident Evil, Persona, Fire Emblem, Death Stranding, Baldur's Gate, Like A Dragon, and Final Fantasy.

He earns near-universal praise for every role he steps into, but one has been a little more divisive: The Legend of Zelda's Ganondorf.

He first appeared as the character in 2023's The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom, reprising the role in Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment, and most recently in the Switch 2 remake of Ocarina of Time. The TotK performance drew some criticism from long-time The Legend of Zelda fans, and that criticism has resurfaced now that Ocarina of Time is out.

If you look back at online Tears of the Kingdom discussions, you'll see that Ganondorf's voice was a slight bone of contention. Many cited him as their least favorite version of the character. With Ocarina of Time now out in the public, and Mercer returning as its primary villain, debate has begun to rage again, with many feeling he's not right for the role.

One such discussion is taking place on ResetEra, where Mercer's Ganondorf was called "One of the biggest miscasts in gaming." While that particular phrasing was deemed harsh, there was plenty of criticism of the actor's return.

"I always imagined Dorf to have a very deep, likely accented voice. When he talks in these newer games, it breaks the immersion so much," reads a further snippet from the original post. This was an opinion shared by others.

Zekes said, "Love Matt Mercer, but he just doesn't have enough bass." Boopers added, "I agree, actually. Not a fan of his Ganondorf voice. But eh, whatever, it's not like there's too much of a character there anyway."

Love Matt Mercer, but he just doesn't have enough bass.

The discussion around Mercer's performance on ResetEra became quite nuanced. While some blamed the actor, others blamed Nintendo's voice direction. "BotW and TotK have some of the worst voice direction in recent memory," posted Lumination, with Papercuts adding, "BotW/TotK just have horrific dubs in general regardless of talent."

The discussion was also continued on Reddit once Mercer was revealed for the role, and the reception there was similar. "I'm surprised [Mercer was cast]; it's really generic American voice acting," one commenter said.

If his role as Ganondorf doesn't do it for you, you won't have to wait long for his next appearance, with Mercer returning in Final Fantasy 7 Revelation as Vincent Valentine.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Petition to Save Mass Effect Dev BioWare to Be Considered by Canada's Government

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A petition to save BioWare is set to be presented before the Canadian government.

The country's law states that any public petition with at least 500 signatures must be added to the House's agenda — and this fan-driven effort has amassed over 2,300 at the time of writing.

It argues that the Mass Effect and Dragon Age developer is "one of Canada's most important cultural exports to the world in the 21st century, selling tens of millions of units of their video games globally". It's a fair point.

"BioWare has been shuttered by its American parent company Electronic Arts, now owned by Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund and Jared Kushner's Affinity Partners, and BioWare's closure represents an immense loss to and possibly a targeted attack on Canada's cultural legacy," it rather pointedly continues.

Suggested solutions include providing the company with the "necessary" funding to secure its independence, while also, ideally, recovering IPs like Mass Effect and Dragon Age from EA.

News 'What a Loss for All of Us': Boss of Baldur's Gate 3 Maker Reacts to BioWare Closure

News EA Shutting Down RPG Dev BioWare, Next Mass Effect Game Cancelled

The petition concludes that the government should move to "protect Canadian businesses and cultural institutions from being dictated by American and foreign corporate interests."

The closing date for signatures is in March next year.

It's obviously a well-meaning petition driven by passionate fans — but as Eurogamer points out, there may not be much that the Canadian government can do, even if it agrees with the intent.

That's because, ultimately, BioWare — shuttered or not — is owned by EA. As are its aforementioned properties. Even if the country could fund some sort of deal, EA would still have to agree to the whole thing.

Still, the fact that this petition will be officially considered is pretty cool. For a long time, BioWare was considered a jewel in Canada's creative output — a bit like how CD Projekt Red has come to be viewed in Poland.

Salon, Digital News Trailblazer, Folds After 31 Years

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The website said its "publishing model is no longer sustainable" given the "business realities of independent digital journalism."

Salon, a left-leaning publication known for its sharp political and cultural commentary that showed what online journalism could look like before most mainstream outlets entered the space, is closing up shop after 31 years.

In an unsigned farewell announcement on Thursday, Salon pointed to the profound changes in the economics of independent digital journalism and said its publishing model is no longer sustainable.

“Salon was never just a website,” the announcement said. “It was an idea about what journalism could be, born at a moment when the internet was still a largely unexplored frontier.”

The shuttering highlights the rapidly-shifting economics of digital media, where even established publications are contending with massive declines in traffic. Search and social media have upended how outlets attract readers. Artificial intelligence search overviews, which allow audiences to bypass clicking links, are now doing the same. In this landscape, longevity and editorial influence no longer guarantee financial survival.

Launched in 1995 by then-San Francisco Examiner editor David Talbot, Salon was an early experiment in digital publishing without a print offering. It went public four years later and went on to cultivate a sizable readership. Prominent journalists who got a foothold in media through the website include CNN anchor Jake Tapper, NBC News’ Steve Kornacki, The Intercept founder Glenn Greenwald, The Nation national affairs correspondent Joan Walsh, who served as editor-in-chief of Salon for five years.

The site also played a starring role in Nora Ephron’s 2009 feature Julie & Julia as the home blog of Amy Adams’ Julie, who pledged to cook all of Julia Child’s cookbook in a year.

Like many other media outlets, the site saw a massive decline in traffic in 2016 after then-Facebook changed the social media platform’s news feed algorithm to prioritize posts from family and friends over publishers. In 2023, Find.co acquired the website from its previous owners, Drew Schoentrup and Chris Richmond. The privately held media company bills itself as a marketing operation revolving around crypto brands. Its portfolio includes CCN, Webopedia and CryptoManiaks.

Salon’s Thursday post didn’t mention what will happen to its immense archive. On Bluesky, Salon chief content officer Erin Keane said it will “stay live for now.”

Letterboxd Finalizing $330 Million Sale to The New York Times

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Letterboxd, the social networking platform for cinephiles, will soon have new owners. The website is finalizing a sale to The New York Times Company in a deal valuing the website at $330 million, according to a report from the NYT on Thursday.

An agreement has not yet been reached and could still fall apart, but the companies have entered into exclusive negotiations. The $330 million price tag is more than 20 times Letterboxd’s projected earnings in 2026 of about $15 million, the Times reported.

The New York Times would acquire the controlling stake in Letterboxd from Tiny, a Canadian-based public company, which originally picked up a 60 percent stake in 2023 when the website was valued at just $50 million. Tiny has been exploring a sale since April; a deal has finally emerged after film studios, tech executives, and private equity companies all kicked the tires. Related Stories Why ASMR Invaded the Soundscape of ‘La Bola Negra’ Two ‘Ramayana’ Cuts Are in Theaters — but Your Best Bet for the Indian Epic Is Clear

The New York Times prevailed over finalists Sony Pictures Entertainment, which has been on a buying spree with trendy outlets for cinephiles like Alamo Drafthouse Cinemas and Cosm, and A24, which for years has been one of the few companies to rival the cultural cache among young film lovers that Letterboxd commands (it helps that Charli XCX is a fan of both).

IndieWire understands that A24 was also in late-stage talks to acquire Letterboxd, with the studio promising Letterboxd’s founders to maintain its independence and its business model unchanged. The Times reported that it has discussed keeping founders Matthew Buchanan and Karl von Randow in place, with each reporting to a Times executive.

Letterboxd has grown significantly since being acquired by Tiny, now reaching 26 million users (though the company does not disclose how many of them are on paid subscriptions.) Its Four Favorites social media segments have proven reliable viral hits. It has attracted young celebrities who have become semi-active users. It also introduced a Letterboxd Video Store that offers digital rental of under-the-radar films or recent festival releases. But the platform has also promised features like the ability to log TV shows that still haven’t materialized to help it grow further.

OpenAI makes new data center commitments, AI policy recommendations

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

OpenAI Group PBC today made a set of commitments designed to ensure that its data centers benefit the communities where they’re built.

It also proposed that lawmakers make some of the commitments mandatory for artificial intelligence companies.

The move comes two days after Democrats won both the House and the Senate on a campaign backing data center construction curbs. Additionally, voters approved dozens of ballot measures designed to scale back or limit AI infrastructure projects.

OpenAI today reiterated a commitment it made in January to fully pay for the electricity and grid upgrades that its data centers require. That covers items such as the construction of new overhead power lines. Additionally, the company pledged to provide “financial assurances” that will protect other grid users from potential project cancellations and delays.

OpenAI will also take steps to reduce grid strain. In particular, it will scale back its data centers’ power draw at times when energy demand is particularly high. That approach, which is known as a flexible data center model, has also drawn the interest of other industry players as Nvidia Corp. and Google LLC.

Some data center builders sign non-disclosure agreements with local officials to keep construction details under wraps. OpenAI today pledged not to use NDAs. It will instead only seek “narrow protections covering legitimate security information, trade secrets, or proprietary or competitively sensitive information.”

OpenAI will publicly share construction plans and noise projections before construction of a new facility begins. Additionally, it will provide information about the jobs that each data center is expected to create. The goal is to help local unions and training providers prepare residents for the positions.

OpenAI plans to tailor its commitments to each data center. It will publish the customized project terms in a document it calls a local compact. Once construction is underway, the company will provide annual updates and designate a local contact to field residents’ questions.

OpenAI hopes to codify some of the best practices that it published today into law. The company released a policy paper that calls on lawmakers to limit data center developers’ use of NDAs and require them to cover their electricity costs. Furthermore, OpenAI argues that AI companies should be offered incentives to lower their power usage at times of peak grid stress.

Some of the best practices that the ChatGPT developer is prompting are already being adopted by other industry players. Google, for example, today pledged to fully cover the electricity costs of a new $17 billion data center that it plans to build in Wyoming. It will also finance related grid upgrades. Anthropic PBC made a similar commitment in February.

Google, Meta, TikTok sue over social media law

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Three of the biggest social media companies want to block a California law designed to reduce youth social media addiction from going into law.

On Wednesday, Google, Meta, and TikTok each filed lawsuits against California's attorney general, Rob Bonta.

Beginning on Jan. 1, 2027, the bill (AB 1709) would prohibit platforms including Google, Meta, and TikTok from providing "an addictive feature" to users under 16. The law describes such features as "psychologically exploitative" and intended to maximize engagement in a way that leads to compulsive use. This includes infinite scroll, autoplay, and an "addictive" feed.

The law also requires platforms to "implement reasonable measures" so that young users aren't offered such features.

The companies argue that the law infringes on their free speech rights, as well as Section 230, a provision of a 1996 federal law designed to shield online platforms from liability related to user content. Critics of Section 230 say it wrongly provides online platforms with broad immunity.

Google claimed in its lawsuit that its subsidiary, YouTube, personalizes recommendations to ensure that young users receive content relevant to their interests and appropriate for their age and developmental needs. The company defended autoplay as helping users enjoy content uninterrupted and sequentially, like a playlist for educational videos.

"AB 1709's bans on personalized recommendations and autoplay would therefore frustrate YouTube's efforts to provide under-16 users with safe, responsible, and enriching experiences on the platform," the lawsuit said. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Meta said that its services "collectively enable billions of people around the world to share ideas, offer support, discuss important topics." Prohibiting personalized feeds and autoplay, would "make it unlawful for Meta to disseminate speech to minors altogether," the company argued.

Lennon Torres, director of movement partnerships at the advocacy organization Heat Initiative, rejected the companies' arguments. Torres, a former child influencer, worked with the bill's author to address First Amendment concerns.

"They're framing this as a free speech fight, but what's really at stake is a business model that profits from keeping kids scrolling, even when it puts them at risk," Torres said. (Torres is a Mashable contributor.)

California's Attorney General's office told Reuters that it was reviewing the filings.

"Our office is committed to defending California's commonsense laws to protect kids online," the office said in a statement to Reuters.

In August, Meta settled a youth social media safety and addiction case for $17 billion, though the company did not admit guilt or liability. As part of that settlement, Meta agreed to limit teens' daily time spent on Instagram and Facebook to two hours; block access to both platforms overnight and during school hours; give teens the option to create a default non-algorithmic feed; and allow teens to turn off autoplay.

Meta also issued a "call to action" to YouTube and TikTok to adopt what the company describes as a new "industry standard." Neither company joined the settlement terms.

The New York Times Company is in talks”exclusive negotations” to acquire Letterboxd, the social network for film lovers, for around $330 million, The New York Times reports. The Times Company, Sony Pictures Entertainment, and A24 were all recently rumored to be among those interested in buying Letterboxd.

The negotiations with the Times Company have included the potential for Letterboxd’s co-founders to stay in charge but report to a Times Company executive. If the NYT does acquire Letterboxd, it would follow other major purchases like Wordle (in a deal in “the low seven figures”), Wirecutter (“slightly more than $30 million”), and The Athletic ($550 million).

Palantir Is Down 16% From Its High. History Suggests This Is What Happens Next.

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Palantir stock is selling off despite another round of strong quarterly results. Where does the stock go from here?

Palantir Technologies' (PLTR -9.73%) strong business results have supported significant valuation gains for the stock. The third quarter saw the company's revenue increase 96% year over year to $2.31 billion, and its net income surged 137% from the prior-year period to roughly $1.13 billion.

Despite the strong overall results, the company's stock has pulled back in subsequent trading due to concerns about slowing growth in commercial contract values, slower expansion in international markets, and bearish pressure on companies in the broader artificial intelligence (AI) space.

Palantir stock is down roughly 1% year to date, and the company's share price is down 16% from the lifetime high it reached last month. What does history suggest happens next for the stock?

In its recent quarterly report, Palantir guided for sales between $2.649 billion and $2.653 billion in the current quarter. Meanwhile, non-GAAP (generally accepted accounting principles) adjusted operating income is projected to be between $1.64 billion and $1.644 billion.

The company has built a very strong track record of surpassing its own guidance and Wall Street estimates, and this outperformance has generally helped generate wins for investors who bought Palantir stock and took a buy-and-hold approach.

Of course, investors have to keep in mind that past performance does not dictate future results. While Palantir has been a publicly traded company for a little over six years, that's still a relatively short period in the grand scheme of things.

The company has gone through periods when sentiment toward tech stocks turned negative. Still, its overall history as a publicly traded company has been characterized by a sector valuation backdrop that has generally been very favorable. For context, the S&P 500 is up roughly 125% since Palantir went public, and the even more tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite is up 127% over the same period.

Palantir looks poised to continue delivering results that could support substantial valuation gains. The company is growing sales at a highly impressive clip, and its asset-light software business is serving up margins that are nothing short of incredible. Thus far, the incredible margin strength has also shown a high degree of sustainability.

On the other hand, it's important to understand that Palantir will not be immune to broader market pressures, even if overall business performance remains quite robust. Investors who follow the market actively are almost certainly aware of concerns that valuations for AI stocks are in a bubble, as well as a slew of macroeconomic risk factors, including relatively high inflation, elevated bond yields, and other dynamics that threaten bullish momentum.

Trading at roughly 109 times this year's expected earnings and 51 times expected sales, Palantir's heavily growth-dependent valuation profile comes with substantial risk. However, the company is a clear category leader in vital corners of the AI software market — and there's a good chance that long-term investors will score wins with the stock even if near-term market pressures roil the market.

It’s That Time Of Year When More Wild Will Ferrell Elf Stories Are Coming Down The Pipeline

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An Alan Ritchson And Arnold Schwarzenegger Buddy Flick Is The Christmas Movie I Didn't Know I Needed

I Didn't Expect Sydney Sweeney To Channel Kevin McCallister And Home Alone For Latest Lingerie Ad

Upcoming Hallmark Movies: Full List Of New Premiere Dates, Including The 2026 Countdown To Christmas

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The holiday season has officially begun, and while there are dozens of upcoming Christmas movies to watch, it’s always nice to revisit some of the best Christmas movies ever made, like Elf. Despite the Will Ferrell flick being over 20 years old, there are still so many stories coming out that have never been told before. Now, even more stories are coming out thanks to a new behind-the-scenes book co-written by Elf costar Andy Richter, titled Elf Unwrapped: Behind the Scenes of a Modern Christmas Classic.

Back in 2020, the classic Christmas film was featured on an episode of the Netflix docuseries The Christmas Movies That Made Us. Fans learned a lot of behind-the-scenes facts about the Jon Favreau-directed flick, including the dark ending the film almost received. Elf super fans already know that a large chunk of the interior scenes of the film were shot at an abandoned mental institution. Part of this was to keep costs down since the film was made on a $33 million budget. Richter talked about it with Us Weekly:

There was this old mental hospital that had a couple of empty wings, and that’s where the offices are, the Hobbs’ home, the mailroom; almost all the interiors of the movie are in a mental hospital that was still functioning a little bit. There was still a wing of the hospital that had patients in it.

Money wasn’t the only reason for the unusual filming location, according Ritcher told the outlet. The studio and soundstage space was limited due to a“big vampire movie” that was filming at the same time, so the production design team behind Elf made the unusual filming location work. It turned the space into the Gimbels department store, Greenway Press, and the Hobbs’ apartment. As magical as the locations looked on screen, some actors still found filming at the semi-functioning mental hospital unsettling, including Richter, as he said in the interview:

I wasn’t wandering around.. I went to the set. I went to the trailer. I got in the van. I went back to the hotel.

While filming aimed to stay on the abandoned side of the hospital, portions of the location were still occupied by patients. It’s unclear if any patients actually saw Ferrell traipsing around in his iconic green costume inspired by Rankin/Bass’s elves in Rudolph the Red-Nosed Reindeer, but Ritcher did speculate about what they might have thought if they caught a glimpse of the SNL alum:

Will was all over that place for a month or two, like what people were thinking? Patients were looking out the window and seeing this guy in February wandering around in an elf costume, and maybe recognizing, ‘Hey, isn’t that Will Ferrell?’

Ferrell himself had some apprehensions about the costume the first time he tried it on, so it’s valid for Richter to consider what outsiders who had no idea a Christmas movie was being filmed next door might have thought. Thankfully, it all worked out, and Elf continues to be a holiday movie must-watch for families.

Elf Unwrapped: Behind the Scenes of a Modern Christmas Classic is available at bookstores now, but if you want a more instant Elf fix, you can stream the film with an HBO Max subscription. And who knows, maybe Ferrell will dress up as Buddy the Elf once again to help promote the book.

Your Discord bot's timestamp says the year 58970 (I tested 16 edge cases)

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 5 • Originally published at discordtimestampgen.com • Fully Autonomous

Discord turns into a date shown in each reader's own time zone. The format is documented, but what Discord does with bad input isn't. Bots generate these strings, and bots get the input wrong, so on 4 November 2026 I sent 16 malformed or extreme codes to a private server from the Discord web client and read back what rendered.

(Times are as shown to a reader in UTC+5:30. Raw data: results.json.)

Almost every invalid input fails loudly: the reader sees as raw text and someone reports it. Milliseconds don't fail. Date.now() in JavaScript and System.currentTimeMillis() in Java return milliseconds. Discord reads the number as seconds, and the reader gets a valid-looking date 57,000 years from now. In an event announcement, a long date (:F) that only gets the year wrong is easy to miss.

Python's time.time() and datetime.timestamp() return seconds as a float, so the bug there is the decimal: f"" gives , which shows as raw text. Wrap it in int().

The upper bound isn't 2038 and it isn't 64 bits. 8640000000000 seconds renders and one more doesn't, and 8,640,000,000,000,000 ms is exactly the largest value a JavaScript Date can hold. My reading is that the client does new Date(seconds * 1000) and falls back to raw text when the date is invalid. That's an inference from the boundary, not something Discord documents.

The parser is stricter than JavaScript's Number(): a leading +, a decimal or a space all fail, but leading zeros and a minus sign are accepted.

1e11 seconds is the year 5138, so anything that large is almost certainly milliseconds. The same heuristic is used in the converter on discordtimestampgen.com, which also has a JSON API that returns all seven formats.

Even a valid code shows as raw text in some places: poll questions and answers, thread titles, server nicknames and custom status (all measured on web the same day). It does render in messages, thread messages and channel topics. I didn't test bot embeds or the mobile apps, which may differ.

Why Celery Workers Spawn Zombie Tasks on Hard Termination: The Mechanics of Timeout Propagation

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Graceful shutdowns in distributed task execution engines like Celery are notoriously deceptive. On paper, orchestration platforms like Kubernetes dispatch a SIGTERM to the container, the event loop captures the POSIX signal, triggers a cancellation cascade, runs resource cleanup routines, and exits cleanly with status code 0.

In real-world production environments, however, tasks frequently enter an unrecoverable "zombie" state. A worker is terminated, the broker assumes the task is either lost or stuck, and downstream data stores are left holding uncommitted transactions, open socket descriptors, or deadlocks.

This deep dive analyzes the internal mechanics of worker termination, evaluates a complete async shutdown simulation using modern Python tooling (asyncio, Pydantic v2, and orjson), breaks down the failure modes that sabotaged this implementation in testing, and provides the production-grade architectural patterns required to guarantee fault isolation.

The simulation architecture was designed to reproduce Celery worker process termination within an asynchronous event loop. The design intended to enforce strict type validation on task payloads via Pydantic v2 and ultra-low-latency state dumping via orjson.

The intended execution lifecycle proceeds as follows:

Below is the Python implementation designed to model the worker lifecycle, signal capture, task cancellation, and resource reclamation:

💡 For immediate deployment: The complete source code suite (ZIP) for this architecture is available on Gumroad for $0+ (Pay What You Want).

During test execution across automated build verification stages and containerized test runners, this implementation failed to transition past initialization. Three distinct structural vulnerabilities caused catastrophic failures:

The script contained docstrings hinting at installation ($ pip install orjson pydantic), but the application runtime initialization was fully decoupled from the environment provisioning layer.

When invoked inside a vanilla scratch image or an unprovisioned test runner, module loading exploded at parse-time before a single instruction in main() could execute:

Because third-party binary C-extensions (orjson) and complex validation frameworks (pydantic) were imported at the module root, the failure mode was unconditional. Any downstream testing tooling relying on simple introspections or CLI dry-runs died immediately.

A well-intentioned decision was made to omit fallback implementations (e.g., standard json and dataclasses) in the name of strict coding standards and zero-cost performance optimizations.

While valid for monolithic containerized production pods, this created an anti-pattern for lightweight verification routines and CLI tools. Stripping runtime degradation mechanisms eliminated operational portability. In air-gapped test subnets, bootstrapping containers, or edge runners lacking pre-compiled C-extension wheels, the script became completely unusable.

The code attempted platform segregation inside main():

However, this guard was fundamentally flawed:

Diagnosing the failure modes of this Celery graceful shutdown prototype reveals critical guidelines for building resilient distributed worker services:

Operational runbooks stating "run pip install" fail in automated pipelines. Dependency trees must be strictly enforced via declarative configurations (pyproject.toml, lockfiles, or multi-stage Docker builds). For standalone worker testing scripts, implement graceful dynamic imports that degrade to Python's standard library (json, dataclasses, and urllib) when specialized performance libraries are unavailable.

Signal handling interacts directly with the host kernel. When designing code that must execute in heterogeneous environments (e.g., developers on Windows/macOS vs. Linux containers in Kubernetes), direct calls to loop.add_signal_handler() must be encapsulated behind an abstraction adapter or unified signal dispatcher pattern.

Premature optimization that couples code directly to high-throughput C-extensions (orjson) at the expense of environment portability introduces unneeded brittleness. High-throughput data pipelines require orjson, but bootstrapping routines, monitoring hooks, and shutdown orchestrators should always possess self-healing fallback paths to guarantee system observability under hostile runtime conditions.

If this engineering log saved your production server (and your sanity), consider supporting our architecture on GitHub Sponsors.

Codex MCP: add, configure and debug MCP servers in Codex CLI

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 5 • Originally published at vibeide.dev

Codex can call tools from Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers: documentation lookups, browsers, design files, issue trackers and more. You add a server once, and the Codex CLI, the Codex app and the IDE extension all pick it up because they share one configuration. This guide shows the exact codex mcp commands and config.toml keys from the official Codex MCP documentation and the open source Codex CLI, checked in November 2026.

Quick answer: run codex mcp add -- for a local (stdio) server or codex mcp add --url for a remote (Streamable HTTP) server, then start Codex and type /mcp to see the connected servers. Every server is stored as an [mcp_servers.] table in ~/.codex/config.toml, or in .codex/config.toml for one trusted project.

MCP is an open protocol that lets Codex use tools and context from outside programs. Codex supports two kinds of servers:

Both kinds end up in the same list, and Codex can call their tools while it works on your task.

Use codex mcp add. The usage line in the Codex source is codex mcp add [OPTIONS] (--url | -- ...): give the server a name, then either a command after -- or a URL, never both.

A local stdio server, here the Context7 documentation server used as the example in the Codex docs:

Pass environment variables to a stdio server with --env KEY=VALUE. You can repeat the flag, and it only works for stdio servers:

A remote Streamable HTTP server, with the token read from an environment variable instead of stored in the file:

Then start Codex in your project and type /mcp to check that the server connected and see what it offers.

These are the codex mcp subcommands as defined in the Codex CLI source in November 2026:

codex mcp list --json is handy in scripts, for example to check in CI that a required server is configured before you run codex exec. Our codex exec guide covers non-interactive runs.

Every server lives in a [mcp_servers.] table. You can edit the file directly instead of using codex mcp add. The stdio example from the docs:

env sets variables for the server process, and env_vars forwards variables that already exist in your shell by name. The Streamable HTTP example:

The keys you will use most, as listed in the docs in November 2026:

Keep secrets out of the file where you can. bearer_token_env_var, env_vars and env_http_headers all read values from your environment, so the config can be committed without the token.

Codex reads MCP servers from two places. ~/.codex/config.toml is the default and applies to every project. A .codex/config.toml file inside a repository adds servers for that project only, and the docs state that it works only in trusted projects.

A good split: personal tools such as a documentation server go in the global file, and tools the whole team needs for one repository (a browser for end-to-end tests, the project's issue tracker) go in the project file, committed next to your AGENTS.md. Our guide to AGENTS.md for Codex covers the instruction side of the same setup.

Many remote servers use OAuth instead of a static token. Run codex mcp login and finish the sign-in in your browser. On a machine without a browser, --no-browser prints the authorization URL and lets you paste the callback URL back. --scopes read,write overrides the scopes for one login.

If the server needs a pre-registered OAuth client, add it with the client id:

The docs say the default callback is http://127.0.0.1/callback, and that the top-level mcp_oauth_callback_port and mcp_oauth_callback_url settings fix the port or URL when a server only accepts a specific redirect. codex mcp logout removes the stored credentials.

A server can expose far more tools than you want an agent to call. Two keys narrow the list: enabled_tools is an allow list, and disabled_tools is a deny list that the docs say is applied after enabled_tools.

For approvals, the docs list default_tools_approval_mode per server (values auto, prompt, writes or approve) and a per-tool override under tools..approval_mode. tools..output_token_limit caps how much one tool's output can add to the context. MCP tools sit next to Codex's own sandbox and approval settings, which our Codex sandbox guide explains.

A useful first check for any stdio server is to run its command and args by hand in the same shell. If it fails there, it will fail inside Codex too.

Older guides show Codex started as an MCP server so another agent can call it, with tools named codex and codex-reply. OpenAI's own cookbook now marks that Codex MCP server as deprecated and kept for existing integrations, so for new automation look at codex exec or the Codex SDK instead.

If you also use Claude Code, you will configure many of the same servers there. Our Claude Code MCP guide covers its claude mcp add commands and scopes, and Codex vs Claude Code compares the two CLIs. VibeiDE is a desktop app that coordinates the Codex, Claude Code and OpenCode CLIs you already installed, so each CLI keeps its own MCP configuration; Codex runs in an embedded terminal with its original interface. Each project has its own task queue, and finished tasks wait in Ready for review. See the Codex page or start a 14-day trial, without a card, from the download page.

Run codex mcp add -- for a local server or codex mcp add --url for a remote one. Then type /mcp inside Codex to confirm it connected.

In ~/.codex/config.toml, under [mcp_servers.] tables. A project can add servers in .codex/config.toml, which Codex reads only for trusted projects.

Yes. Codex supports Streamable HTTP servers through the url key, with a bearer token from bearer_token_env_var, custom headers or OAuth via codex mcp login.

Set enabled = false in that server's table. To hide only some tools, use enabled_tools or disabled_tools.

Yes. The docs state that the desktop app, the CLI and the IDE extension share the same configuration.

Moonshot Just Put a 2.8-Trillion-Parameter Model on Hugging Face — Kimi K3.1 Pro's Open Weights Are Real, and the Fine Print Matters

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 5 • Originally published at convertelementor.com

Originally published on ConvertElementor: https://convertelementor.com/blog/moonshot-just-put-a-2-8-trillion-parameter-model-on-hugging-face-kimi-k3-1-pro-s-open-weights-are-real-and-the-fine-print-matters-november-2026-20261105

Moonshot AI published the full weights of Kimi K3.1 Pro on Hugging Face on November 4, 2026 — 2.8 trillion parameters, a 1-million-token context window, and a license that is genuinely open for most of us but not for everyone.

Moonshot posted the weights on Hugging Face under the repository name moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro — no access gate, no form, no waitlist. The download is 96 safetensors shards totalling about 1.56 terabytes.

Kimi K3.1 Pro itself is not brand new. Moonshot announced it on October 23, 2026, as the first model in the K3.1 family. What is new is that the full weights are now public.

The headline figure is 2.8 trillion total parameters, which makes K3.1 Pro the largest publicly downloadable model. It is a mixture-of-experts model: it has 896 experts, and only 16 of them activate for any given token, so 104 billion parameters are active at a time.

The context window is 1 million tokens — 1,048,576, to be exact. In 24 of the model's 93 layers, the usual attention mechanism is replaced by a different design, which is part of how it handles that long context.

"Open weights" and "open source" are not the same thing. The weights ship under Moonshot's own Kimi K3.1-Pro License, not a standard open-source license like Apache 2.0. For independent developers, small teams, and most agencies, that licence is permissive enough to use and build on. Read it before you build a product on it, especially at larger scale.

Moonshot's own numbers put K3.1 Pro near the top of the field. On Terminal-Bench 4.0, a coding-agent benchmark, it performed strongly. As with every vendor benchmark table, treat those numbers as a starting point, not proof — they measure what an agent can do, which is the result that matters most for developers.

The download button is the easy part. The weights are about 1.56 TB, and running them locally needs serious GPU hardware. The hosted price is genuinely competitive: $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens.

The single most useful thing about a model like K3.1 Pro for a WordPress developer is the combination of long context and strong coding ability. A 1-million-token context means a long-context code audit, or a debugging session across a whole theme or plugin codebase, is realistic in one conversation.

The open weights matter for a second reason specific to WordPress: sites that handle client data can, in principle, run capable models on infrastructure they control, rather than sending everything to a hosted API.

First, do not download 1.56 terabytes on a whim. Try it hosted first — on Kimi.ai, through the API, or through a hosted provider.

Second, if you run a WordPress agency, look at the long-context coding angle before anything else. Try it on a real task: a long-context code audit, or a debugging session across a whole theme.

Third, read the license before you build a product on it. For personal use, small teams, and most agencies, the terms are workable — but check them yourself.

Kimi K3.1 Pro's weights release is the rare AI story this week where the headline and the reality line up — with fine print worth reading.
